Local search strategies:
1. **Simplex subgradient on p with sum=1**:
   For current p (normalized), compute c=conv(p,p). Choose active peaks: top K largest coefficients, e.g., K=10 or K=ceil(0.05n). But for large n top K computations cheap if indices known. Compute gradient for active weights w_k proportional to exp(λ c_k) maybe to smooth max. For Newton? We can implement smoothing objective F_λ = (1/λ) log ∑ exp(λ c_k) - constant? Gradient: g_i = ∑_k r_k * 2 p_{k-i} where r_k=exp(λ c_k)/∑exp. This smooths max and allows using all k with weights. But computing r over n^2? c length 2n-1, n up to4096; length8191. λ * c values maybe max M~0.0003? Wait normalized p sum1, c max ~score/(2n) ~1.5/(2n) ~1e-4 for n=4096. λ needs ~10000 to approximate max. expλc can overflow range: c min 0 to max1e-4; times λ=??? Could use max shift. But gradient of logsumexp: r_k=exp(λ(c_k-cmax))/Z. For canonical max, λ=50? c range over n maybe small; top coefficients within few 1e-5; λ=1000. We can compute. The gradient vector length 3n-1. To compute grad = 2∑ r_k p_{k-i} weighted. We can do via convolution: Create array r of length 2n-1 (weights). Need for i=0..n-1 grad_i = ∑_{k} r_k p_{k-i}. Let p_pad? This is cross-correlation: corr(k,i). We can compute as `np.convolve(r, p)` but alignment. conv(r,p)[i + (n-1)]? Let's derive: conv(r,p)_t = ∑_{u} r_u p_{t-u}, with p out of range zero. For t = i + (n-1), ∑_u r_u p_{i+n-1-u}. We want ∑_k r_k p_{k-i}. Set u = k, t-u = k-i => t=k? Not constant. Try reverse p: cross-correlation c_i = ∑_k r_k p_{k-i}. Let q_j = p_{j}; c_i = (r * q_reversed?) If q_rev[t]=p_{n-1-t}. conv(r,q_rev)_? Might be easier to compute directly for top K active rather than all r. If using smooth weights, length 8191, could compute via FFT? Or direct with np.convolve: We want for each i, sum over k of r_k p_{k-i}. This is the convolution of r with reversed p? Let s_i = p_{n-1-i}. Then (r * s)_{i+n-1} =∑_u r_u s_{i+n-1-u}=∑_u r_u p_{u-i}? since s_{...}=p_{n-1-(i+n-1-u)}=p_{u-i}. Yes with u=k. Great. grad_i = 2*conv(r, p_rev)[i+n-1], for i=0..n-1. Good. p_rev = p[::-1]. conv length 3n-1. We can compute r exactly length maybe 2n-1 (or embed zeros). `np.convolve(r, p_rev)` returns length len(r)+n-1 =3n-1. But p_rev length n. Yes slice [n-1:2n-1]. For r length 2n-1 with index offset 0..2n-2, formula? conv index t = (n-1)+i corresponds u? We used s index i+n-1-u; for t=n-1 + i. Good. Works. So gradient smooth max cheap via one convolution O(n^2) (or FFT). But we already compute c; convolve r with p_rev extra O(n^2). Could combine? For large n, maybe too heavy if many steps. For small n, fine. For n=4096, 16M extra per step; if 1000 steps 16B too much. But local search can focus on smaller n or limited steps.
   After gradient, descent direction d = grad - mean(grad) (to keep sum approx). Step η. Since c_k scale with p, objective scale invariant, moving along sum-preserving. p_new = p - η d; clip to 0; normalize. Choose η adaptively via line search: try a list of steps maybe based on gradient norm. For normalized p, gradient values ~2∑r p ~O(1). Scale of d ~O(1). M ~1/n. To change p significantly need many steps. Could use accelerated? Maybe use p_new = p * exp(-η d) (multiplicative) then normalize; this preserves positivity better. d as relative gradient? If using multiplicative, take log p; gradient w.r.t log p_i = p_i ∂M/∂p_i (plus sum constraint). Then p_i ← p_i exp(-η p_i grad_i)? Hmm.
   Simpler: block scaling local search might be faster.
2. **Active peak pair scaling**:
   If peak at k, terms p_i p_{k-i}. We can reduce these by scaling down p_i for i in peak and scaling up indices not involved. For normalized p, choose vector v: For active max set, v_i positive contribution. We want p_new = (1-t)p + t q where q has lower gradient. One approach: set q_i ∝ p_i * exp(-η * grad_smooth_i) (softmax update). This is natural exponential tilting: new score maybe lower if grad_smooth estimates max direction. This is like minimizing exponential utility. We can implement: p_new = p * exp(-eta * smooth_grad - average?) then normalize. The smooth_grad = 2∑ r_k p_{k-i}. To reduce contributions to high c, large grad_i should be decreased. Multiplicative update with sum=1: p_new ∝ p_i exp(-eta grad_i). This is like Sinkhorn? For small eta. We can line search eta from list: [0.01,0.02,...0.5]. Because p normalized; grad ~1, eta moderate. This can flatten peaks. It's positive. It uses convolution for grad each step.
   But if using full logsumexp weights, grad smooth. If top peaks only, choose r uniform over top K. We can use r = softmax(λ c) with λ heuristic: Since max-min c difference maybe O(1/n), set λ = 50*n? Wait c normalized max ~M_dm/n? For score ~1.5, M_dim~0.75, cmax~0.75/n. For n=100, cmax=.0075. Difference of coefficients maybe .001. To focus top max, λ maybe 1000? We can just use top K: r zeros except top K largest c uniformly. Then grad exact subgradient (average over top peaks). This tends to lower all top peaks. Good.

Implementation details:

Block scale line search implementation:
```
def block_scale_candidate(p, C, start, L, factors, sum_p):
    n = p.size
    b = p[start:start+L]
    if b.size==0 or not finite: return
    bsum = b.sum()
    cross = np.convolve(p, b)  # length n+L-1
    dd = np.convolve(b, b)     # length 2L-1
    end = min(2*n-1, start + n + L - 1)
    aff_len = end - start
    C_aff = C[start:end]
    cross_aff = cross[:aff_len]
    dd_aff = np.zeros(aff_len)
    dd_len = min(len(dd), aff_len)
    dd_aff[:dd_len] += dd[:dd_len]
    # outside max
    outmax=0.0
    if start>0: outmax=max(outmax, np.max(C[:start]))
    if end < C.size: outmax=max(outmax, np.max(C[end:]))
    best_alpha=0; best_score=parent score? But parent_score = 2n*max(C)/sum_p^2. We can compute for factors.
    for f in factors:
       alpha=f-1
       if alpha ==0: continue
       m_aff = np.max(C_aff + 2.0*alpha*cross_aff + alpha*alpha*dd_aff)
       m = max(m_aff, outmax)
       ssum = sum_p + alpha*bsum
       if ssum <=1e-12: continue
       score = 2*n*m/(ssum*ssum)
       if score < best_score: ...
    return best F and score maybe
```

```
def local_search_power(p, C, score, rng, deadline, max_updates=100, label=''):
   n = p.size
   parent_score = score
   sum_p = float(np.sum(p)) # should 1
   # Ensure C normalized consistent with sum_p: if not, C=conv(p,p); score=...
   # choose factors
   factors = FACTORS  # global list maybe
   # maybe adapt maxL
   minL = max(1, min(8, n//100))
   maxL = max(minL+1, min(n//2, int(min(128, max(4, n*0.05)))))
   no_improve =0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # choose block
       if rng.random()<0.7:
          kmax = int(np.argmax(C))
          lo = max(0, kmax - n + 1)
          hi = min(n-1, kmax)
          i0 = int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1))
          L = chooseL
          if rng.random()<0.5: start = max(0,min(n-L, i0 - L//2))
          else: j0 = max(0, min(n-1, kmax - i0)); start = ... around j0
       else:
          L=chooseL; start = rng.integers(0,n-L+1)
       improvement = try_block_scale(...)
       if improved:
          # update arrays, score, no_improve=0
          if new_score < global_best? We return local best; outside updates global.
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > 200: random_escape()
       if time near deadline break
   return p,C,score
```

Implementation of `try_block_scale` with factors:
```
def try_block_scale(p, C, start, L, factors, parent_score, sum_p):
    n=p.size
    if start<0 or start+L>n or L<=0: return None
    b = p[start:start+L]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)): return None
    bsum = float(b.sum())
    if bsum <= 0: # block zero; scaling no effect except if f>0? If b zero and d zero, no change. return None
    # But if b all zero, scaling zero no effect; random noise needed.
    cross = np.convolve(p, b)  # length n+L-1
    dd = np.convolve(b, b)
    end = min(2*n-1, start + n + L - 1)
    aff = end - start
    if aff <=0: return None
    C_aff = C[start:end]
    cross_aff = cross[:aff]
    dd_aff = np.zeros(aff, dtype=np.float64)
    dlen = min(len(dd), aff)
    dd_aff[:dlen] += dd[:dlen]
    outmax=0.0
    if start>0:
       m=np.max(C[:start]); if m>outmax: outmax=m
    if end < C.size:
       m=np.max(C[end:]); ...
    best_alpha = None
    best_score = parent_score
    # include current? parent_score maybe exact; no need
    for f in FACTORS:
       if f <= 0: alpha=-1 else alpha=f-1
       if alpha ==0: continue
       # If f extremely large? no
       m_aff = np.max(C_aff + (2.0*alpha)*cross_aff + (alpha*alpha)*dd_aff)
       m_all = m_aff if m_aff>outmax else outmax
       ssum = sum_p + alpha*bsum
       if ssum <= 1e-12: continue
       sc = (2.0*n*m_all)/(ssum*ssum)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=sc; best_alpha=alpha
    return None if best_alpha is None else (1+best_alpha, best_score, best_alpha, cross, dd, bsum)
```
But if best_alpha is None, cross/dd discarded. If best, we need update p and C. We can reconstruct C_new after:
```
def apply_block_scale(p,C,start,L,alpha,C?) cross,dd,bsum:
   C_new = C.copy()
   add_shifted_scaled(C_new, cross, 2*alpha, start)
   add_shifted_scaled(C_new, dd, alpha*alpha, start)
   p[start:start+L] *= (1+alpha)
   return C_new
```

```
end = min(dst.size, offset + src.size)
if end>offset and scale!=0:
   dst[offset:end] += scale*src[:end-offset]
```

```
def convolve_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    m=1
    while m < 2*n-1: m <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, m)[:2*n-1]
    c[c<0] = 0? maybe small negative
    return c
```

Let's detail helper functions top-level:
```
def norm_power(n, beta):
    x = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64) ** (-beta)
    x /= x.sum()
    return x

def score_conv(p):
    c = np.convolve(p, p)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    n = p.size
    if s <= 1e-12: return np.inf, c
    return float(2*n*np.max(c)/(s*s)), c

def add_shifted_scaled(dst, src, scale, offset): ...
```

Let's design `local_search` more concretely:
```
def local_search(p, C, score, deadline, rng, label):
    n = p.size
    minL = max(1, min(4, n//100))
    maxL = max(minL, min(n//2, int(min(128, max(4, n*0.03)))))
    no_imp = 0
    last_imp = time.monotonic()
    iterations=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       iterations += 1
       # choose L
       L = int(rng.integers(minL, maxL + 1))
       # choose start
       r = rng.random()
       if r < 0.7:
          kmax = int(np.argmax(C))
          lo, hi = valid range
          i0 = int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1))
          if rng.random()<0.5:
             center = i0
          else:
             center = max(0,min(n-1, kmax - i0))
          start = int(rng.integers(max(0, center - maxL//2), min(n-L, center + maxL//2) + 1))
       else:
          start = int(rng.integers(0, n-L + 1))
       res = try_block_scale(...)
       if res is not None:
           f, C_new, sc, sum_s = res? Build in local maybe.
           accept
           no_imp=0; last_imp=...; print
       else:
           no_imp +=1
           if no_imp > 300 and n <= 4096 and rng.random()<0.05:
               res2 = try_global_noise...
               if res2 improved: accept; no_imp=0
               else: no_imp=0 maybe? 
           if no_imp > 2000: reset p to power at beta? But we don't know beta; could generate noise power with n.
    return p,C,score
```
Need `try_block_scale` currently only returns best f/score, not C_new. To avoid recomputing cross for building, maybe return cross,dd if improved? But cross/dd arrays length n+L,2L; returning and then building O(n) okay. We can just recompute cross/dd inside `apply`? That doubles cost. Better `try_block_scale` can when improved directly build C_new and return it. It already has cross and dd. Let's implement:
```
def try_block_scale(...):
   ... find best_f, best_score
   if best_score >= parent_score: return None
   alpha=best_f-1
   C_new = C.copy()
   add_shifted_scaled(C_new, cross, 2*alpha, start)
   add_shifted_scaled(C_new, dd, alpha*alpha, start)
   sum_s = sum_p + alpha*bsum
   return best_f, C_new, best_score, sum_s
```

`try_block_noise` generic with d relative block:
```
def try_block_noise(p,C,start,L,sigma,parent_score,sum_p,rng):
   b = p[start:start+L].copy()
   q = np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,L)) * b
   q = np.clip(q,0,1000)
   d = q - b
   if not np.any(d): return None
   cross = np.convolve(p,d)
   dd = np.convolve(d,d)
   end = min(2n-1, start+n+L-1)
   aff=end-start
   C_aff=C[start:end]
   cross_aff=cross[:aff]
   dd_aff=zeros; ...
   outmax=...
   m_aff = np.max(C_aff + 2*cross_aff + dd_aff)
   m = max(m_aff,outmax)
   ssum=sum_p+d.sum()
   if ssum<=0 return None
   sc=2n*m/sum_s^2
   if sc < parent_score:
       C_new=C.copy(); add...; p new block? We need q.
       return sc,C_new,ssum,q
   return None
```
Use after stalemate. Note if d has negative values and q nonnegative, formula exact. Good.
But if d has large negative? q≥0 so d≥-b.

Could also use **shifted block** to add mass from low to high: e.g., transfer block to another. But not necessary.

Now, code implementation of truncation:
```
def consider_truncations(p, best_by_n, global variables):
    n=p.size
    fracs = [0.15,0.25,0.4,0.55,0.7,0.85,0.95]
    for f in fracs:
       m=int(n*f)
       for arr in (p[:m], p[n-m:]):
          q=arr/arr.sum()
          C,s=score_conv(q)
          update if improves
```

```
def update_global_state(state, score, p, C, n, beta):
```

Let's produce final code. We'll include docstring.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-function coefficient sequence minimizing

    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a)^2)

The search combines:
  * analytic one-sided power-law families a_i ~ (i+1)^(-beta),
  * asymmetric two-sided mixtures of those families,
  * exact incremental local search using fast block-scale rescalings,
  * truncation / support reduction,
  * final validation with the official evaluate_sequence().

The code is self-contained, uses only numpy and time, and returns the best
sequence found before the deadline.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


BLOCK_FACTORS = (
    0.0, 0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.65,
    0.80, 0.90, 0.95, 0.98, 1.02, 1.05, 1.10, 1.20,
    1.35, 1.55, 1.85, 2.30, 3.00
)


def power_sequence(n: int, beta: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Normalized positive power-law sequence a_i ~ (i+1)^(-beta)."""
    n = int(n)
    if n < 1:
        n = 1
    x = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64) ** (-float(beta))
    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        x = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0
    return x / s


def mixed_power_sequence(n: int, beta: float, w: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Normalized mixture of decreasing and increasing power laws."""
    n = int(n)
    if n < 1:
        n = 1
    left = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64) ** (-float(beta))
    right = np.arange(n, 0, -1, dtype=np.float64) ** (-float(beta))
    ww = float(w)
    x = ww * left + (1.0 - ww) * right
    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        x = left
        s = float(np.sum(x))
    return x / s


def score_conv(p: np.ndarray):
    """Internal exact score and full convolution for nonnegative p."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.convolve(p, p)
    mc = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mc) or mc < 0.0:
        mc = 0.0
    sc = float(2.0 * n * mc / (s * s))
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        sc = np.inf
    return sc, c


def add_shifted_scaled(dst: np.ndarray, src: np.ndarray, scale: float, offset: int) -> None:
    """Add scale * src into dst[offset : offset+len(src)], with safe trimming."""
    if scale == 0.0 or src.size == 0:
        return
    start = int(offset)
    if start >= dst.size:
        return
    end = min(dst.size, start + src.size)
    if end <= start:
        return
    dst[start:end] += scale * src[:end - start]


def update_by_n(best_by_n: dict, n: int, p: np.ndarray, c: np.ndarray, score: float) -> None:
    """Keep the best candidate for each length n."""
    if score >= np.inf or not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    n = int(n)
    if p is None or c is None:
        return
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)) or not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
        return
    prev = best_by_n.get(n)
    if prev is None or score < prev[0]:
        best_by_n[n] = [float(score), p.copy(), c.copy()]


def update_global_state(state, score: float, p: np.ndarray, c: np.ndarray, n: int, beta: float) -> None:
    """Update a [score, p, c, n, beta] container in place."""
    if score >= np.inf or not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    if p is None or c is None:
        return
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)) or not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
        return
    if score < state[0]:
        state[0] = float(score)
        state[1] = p.copy()
        state[2] = c.copy()
        state[3] = int(n)
        state[4] = float(beta)


def candidate_key(item):
    """Sort key for (n, [score,p,c])."""
    return item[1][0]


def try_block_scale(p: np.ndarray, c_parent: np.ndarray, start: int, length: int,
                    parent_score: float, sum_p: float):
    """
    Try multiplicatively scaling one contiguous block by factors in BLOCK_FACTORS.

    Exact incremental updating:
       q = p except q[start:start+L] = f * p[start:start+L]
       c(q) = c(p) + 2 * conv(p, d) + conv(d, d), where d is the block change.

    Returns None, or (f, c_new, score, sum_q).
    """
    n = int(p.size)
    start = int(start)
    length = int(length)
    if length <= 0 or start < 0 or start + length > n:
        return None

    block = p[start:start + length]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(block)):
        return None
    bsum = float(np.sum(block))
    if bsum <= 1e-15:
        return None

    # Relative length-L block change when block is scaled by factor f:
    # d = (f - 1) * block. The functions of alpha = f - 1 are:
    #   c = c_parent + 2*alpha*cross + alpha^2*dd
    cross = np.convolve(p, block)       # length n + L - 1
    dd = np.convolve(block, block)      # length 2L - 1

    end = min(2 * n - 1, start + n + length - 1)
    aff_len = end - start
    if aff_len <= 0:
        return None

    c_aff = np.asarray(c_parent[start:end], dtype=np.float64).copy()

    cross_aff = np.zeros(aff_len, dtype=np.float64)
    m = min(cross.size, aff_len)
    cross_aff[:m] = cross[:m]

    dd_aff = np.zeros(aff_len, dtype=np.float64)
    m = min(dd.size, aff_len)
    dd_aff[:m] = dd[:m]

    outmax = 0.0
    if start > 0:
        v = float(np.max(c_parent[:start]))
        if v > outmax:
            outmax = v
    if end < c_parent.size:
        v = float(np.max(c_parent[end:]))
        if v > outmax:
            outmax = v

    best_f = None
    best_score = parent_score

    def eval_factor(f: float) -> float:
        f = float(f)
        if f < 0.0:
            return np.inf
        alpha = f - 1.0
        if abs(alpha) < 1e-12:
            return parent_score
        ssum = sum_p + alpha * bsum
        if ssum <= 1e-12:
            return np.inf
        val = c_aff + (2.0 * alpha) * cross_aff + (alpha * alpha) * dd_aff
        mav = float(np.max(val))
        if outmax > mav:
            mav = outmax
        if mav <= 0.0:
            return 0.0
        return float(2.0 * n * mav / (ssum * ssum))

    for f in BLOCK_FACTORS:
        sc = eval_factor(f)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_f = float(f)

    # Local refinement around the best coarse factor.
    if best_f is not None:
        fine = []

        def add_fine(x: float) -> None:
            x = float(x)
            if x < 0.0 or not np.isfinite(x):
                return
            for y in fine:
                if abs(y - x) < 1e-6:
                    return
            if abs(x - 1.0) > 1e-6:
                fine.append(x)

        add_fine(best_f * 0.80)
        add_fine(best_f * 0.90)
        add_fine(best_f * 1.10)
        add_fine(best_f * 1.25)
        if best_f < 0.25:
            add_fine(0.0)
            add_fine(0.01)
            add_fine(0.02)
            add_fine(0.05)
        else:
            add_fine(best_f * 0.95)
            add_fine(best_f * 1.05)

        for f in fine:
            sc = eval_factor(f)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = sc
                best_f = float(f)

    if best_f is None:
        return None

    alpha = float(best_f) - 1.0
    c_new = c_parent.copy()
    add_shifted_scaled(c_new, cross, 2.0 * alpha, start)
    add_shifted_scaled(c_new, dd, alpha * alpha, start)
    np.clip(c_new, 0.0, None, out=c_new)
    sum_q = float(sum_p + alpha * bsum)
    if sum_q <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return (float(best_f), c_new, float(best_score), sum_q)


def try_block_noise(p: np.ndarray, c_parent: np.ndarray, start: int, length: int,
                    parent_score: float, sum_p: float, rng, sigma: float):
    """
    Try a random multiplicative / floor perturb of one block.

    Returns None, or (c_new, score, sum_q, new_block).
    """
    n = int(p.size)
    start = int(start)
    length = int(length)
    if length <= 0 or start < 0 or start + length > n:
        return None

    block = p[start:start + length].copy()
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(block)):
        return None

    pmax = float(np.max(p)) if p.size else 1.0
    floor = max(1e-14, 1e-7 * pmax)

    noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=length))
    new_block = block * noise
    new_block = np.maximum(new_block, floor)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(new_block)):
        return None

    d = new_block - block
    if not np.any(d != 0.0):
        return None

    cross = np.convolve(p, d)
    dd = np.convolve(d, d)

    end = min(2 * n - 1, start + n + length - 1)
    aff_len = end - start
    if aff_len <= 0:
        return None

    c_aff = np.asarray(c_parent[start:end], dtype=np.float64).copy()
    cross_aff = np.zeros(aff_len, dtype=np.float64)
    m = min(cross.size, aff_len)
    cross_aff[:m] = cross[:m]
    dd_aff = np.zeros(aff_len, dtype=np.float64)
    m = min(dd.size, aff_len)
    dd_aff[:m] = dd[:m]

    outmax = 0.0
    if start > 0:
        v = float(np.max(c_parent[:start]))
        if v > outmax:
            outmax = v
    if end < c_parent.size:
        v = float(np.max(c_parent[end:]))
        if v > outmax:
            outmax = v

    val = c_aff + 2.0 * cross_aff + dd_aff
    mav = float(np.max(val))
    if outmax > mav:
        mav = outmax
    dsum = float(np.sum(d))
    ssum = sum_p + dsum
    if ssum <= 1e-12:
        return None
    sc = float(2.0 * n * mav / (ssum * ssum))
    if not np.isfinite(sc) or sc >= parent_score - 1e-12:
        return None

    c_new = c_parent.copy()
    add_shifted_scaled(c_new, cross, 2.0, start)
    add_shifted_scaled(c_new, dd, 1.0, start)
    np.clip(c_new, 0.0, None, out=c_new)
    return (c_new, sc, ssum, new_block)


def local_search(p: np.ndarray, c_parent: np.ndarray, score: float,
                 deadline: float, rng, label: str = ""):
    """
    Exact local hill-climbing using block-scale rescalings and occasional
    block-noise moves.  p and c_parent are modified copies.
    """
    n = int(p.size)
    if n < 2:
        return p, c_parent, score

    # Keep p and c_parent internally normalized, but allow transient unnormalized trial.
    ps = float(np.sum(p))
    if ps > 0.0:
        p = p / ps
        c_parent = c_parent / (ps * ps)
        score = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c_parent)))

    sum_p = 1.0

    minL = max(1, min(4, n // 100))
    maxL = max(minL, min(n // 2, int(min(160, max(6, n * 0.03)))))

    no_improve = 0
    iterations = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iterations += 1

        # Choose block length.
        L = int(rng.integers(minL, maxL + 1))

        # Choose block start: mostly guided by current top convolution coefficient.
        if rng.random() < 0.72:
            kmax = int(np.argmax(c_parent))
            lo = max(0, kmax - n + 1)
            hi = min(n - 1, kmax)
            if lo > hi:
                lo, hi = 0, n - 1
            i0 = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
            if rng.random() < 0.55:
                center = i0
            else:
                center = kmax - i0
                if center < 0 or center >= n:
                    center = i0
        else:
            center = int(rng.integers(0, n))

        low = max(0, center - L)
        high = min(n - L, center)
        if low > high:
            start = max(0, min(n - L, center - L // 2))
        else:
            start = int(rng.integers(low, high + 1))
        start = max(0, min(start, n - L))

        res = try_block_scale(p, c_parent, start, L, score, sum_p)
        if res is not None:
            f, c_new, sc_new, sum_q = res
            p[start:start + L] *= f
            if sum_q <= 1e-12:
                # Should not happen for nonnegative block, but resync to be safe.
                p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
                p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
                ps = float(np.sum(p))
                if ps <= 1e-12:
                    return p, c_parent, score
                p = p / ps
                c_new, sc_new = score_conv(p)
                sum_q = 1.0
                if not np.isfinite(sc_new):
                    return p, c_parent, score
            else:
                p = p / sum_q
                c_new = c_new / (sum_q * sum_q)
                np.clip(c_new, 0.0, None, out=c_new)
                sc_new = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c_new)))
                if not np.isfinite(sc_new):
                    sc_new = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c_parent))) / 0.0
                    if not np.isfinite(sc_new):
                        sc_new = score

            p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            c_parent = np.asarray(c_new, dtype=np.float64)
            sum_p = 1.0
            score = sc_new
            no_improve = 0

            if time.monotonic() - last_print > 5.0 or computation < 3:
                pass
            last_print = time.monotonic()
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 350 and rng.random() < 0.12:
                sigma = float(0.15 + 0.55 * rng.random())
                resn = try_block_noise(p, c_parent, start, L, score, sum_p, rng, sigma)
                if resn is not None:
                    c_new, sc_new, sum_q, new_block = resn
                    p[start:start + L] = new_block
                    if sum_q <= 1e-12:
                        return p, c_parent, score
                    p = p / sum_q
                    c_new = c_new / (sum_q * sum_q)
                    np.clip(c_new, 0.0, None, out=c_new)
                    sc_new = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c_new)))
                    if np.isfinite(sc_new):
                        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
                        c_parent = np.asarray(c_new, dtype=np.float64)
                        sum_p = 1.0
                        score = sc_new
                        no_improve = 0
                        last_print = time.monotonic()

        if no_improve > 5000:
            # Periodically abandon the current basin by taking a small exact
            # perturb and restarting the counter.
            p = np.maximum(p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=n)), 0.0)
            ps = float(np.sum(p))
            if ps > 0.0:
                p = p / ps
                c_parent, score2 = score_conv(p)
                if np.isfinite(score2):
                    c_parent = np.asarray(c_parent, dtype=np.float64)
                    sum_p = 1.0
            no_improve = 0

    return p, c_parent, float(score)


def try_truncation_candidates(p: np.ndarray, best_by_n: dict, state) -> None:
    """Try shorter prefixes/suffixes of p, which can improve n-dependence."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n < 4:
        return
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        return
    fractions = (0.15, 0.25, 0.35, 0.45, 0.55, 0.65, 0.75, 0.85, 0.92, 0.97)
    seen_m = set()
    for fr in fractions:
        m = int(max(2, round(n * fr)))
        if m in seen_m or m >= n or m < 2:
            continue
        seen_m.add(m)
        for seg in (p[:m], p[n - m:]):
            seg = np.asarray(seg, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            s = float(np.sum(seg))
            if s <= 1e-12:
                continue
            q = seg / s
            c, sc = score_conv(q)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                update_by_n(best_by_n, m, q, c, sc)
                update_global_state(state, sc, q, c, m, state[4])


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Generate the best sequence found within the allowed runtime.
    """
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    try:
        total_budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        total_budget = 2400.0
    deadline = start_time + max(10.0, total_budget - 12.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Container for global best: [score, p, c, n, beta]
    global_state = [np.inf, None, None, 0, 0.55]
    best_by_n = {}

    # ---------- Very safe initial candidates ----------
    init_points = [
        (32, 0.50), (48, 0.52), (64, 0.50), (64, 0.53),
        (96, 0.52), (128, 0.50), (128, 0.53), (128, 0.55),
        (192, 0.52), (256, 0.52), (256, 0.54), (512, 0.53),
        (512, 0.55), (1024, 0.53), (2048, 0.53)
    ]
    for n0, b0 in init_points:
        p = power_sequence(n0, b0)
        c, sc = score_conv(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            update_by_n(best_by_n, n0, p, c, sc)
            update_global_state(global_state, sc, p, c, n0, b0)

    # ---------- Optionally use any provided prior construction ----------
    try:
        hraw = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        hraw = None
    if hraw is not None:
        try:
            h = np.asarray(hraw, dtype=np.float64)
            if h.ndim == 1 and 2 <= h.size <= 8000:
                h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
                h = np.minimum(h, 1000.0)
                hs = float(np.sum(h))
                if hs > 1e-12:
                    p = h / hs
                    c, sc = score_conv(p)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        update_by_n(best_by_n, p.size, p, c, sc)
                        update_global_state(global_state, sc, p, c, p.size, float(global_state[4]))
        except Exception:
            pass

    # ---------- Coarse long-capacity sweep over power-law family ----------
    # These lengths cover the usual good regime; the largest are included
    # because the problem's supplied construction has length 4498.
    n_values = [
        8, 10, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20, 24, 28, 32, 40, 48,
        56, 64, 80, 96, 112, 128, 160, 192, 224, 256,
        320, 384, 448, 512, 640, 768, 896, 1024,
        1280, 1536, 1792, 2048, 2560, 3072, 4096, 4498
    ]
    beta_values = np.arange(0.40, 0.81 + 1e-9, 0.01)

    outer_done = False
    for n in n_values:
        if outer_done or time.monotonic() > deadline - 45:
            break
        for beta in beta_values:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 45:
                outer_done = True
                break
            p = power_sequence(n, beta)
            c, sc = score_conv(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                update_by_n(best_by_n, n, p, c, sc)
                update_global_state(global_state, sc, p, c, n, beta)

    # ---------- Focus/coarse refine around current best beta for promising lengths ----------
    items = sorted(best_by_n.items(), key=candidate_key)
    if len(items) > 0:
        top_ns = [int(n) for n, _ in items[:10]]
        best_beta = float(global_state[4])
        if not np.isfinite(best_beta) or best_beta <= 0.35:
            best_beta = 0.53

        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120:
            lo = max(0.40, best_beta - 0.06)
            hi = min(0.80, best_beta + 0.06)
            if lo > hi - 1e-7:
                hi = min(0.80, lo + 0.06)
            fine_betas = np.arange(lo, hi + 1e-9, 0.002)
            for n in top_ns:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60:
                    break
                for beta in fine_betas:
                    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60:
                        break
                    p = power_sequence(n, beta)
                    c, sc = score_conv(p)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        update_by_n(best_by_n, n, p, c, sc)
                        update_global_state(global_state, sc, p, c, n, beta)

        # Also try nearby lengths around the current best length.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 90:
            bns = [float(global_state[3])] if int(global_state[3]) >= 2 else [128.0, 256.0, 512.0]
            for base_n in bns:
                for factor in (0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 1.10, 1.25, 1.45):
                    m = int(max(8, round(base_n * factor)))
                    if m > 6000:
                        continue
                    for beta in (best_beta - 0.02, best_beta, best_beta + 0.02, 0.52, 0.54, 0.56):
                        if not (0.35 <= beta <= 0.85):
                            continue
                        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 45:
                            break
                        p = power_sequence(m, beta)
                        c, sc = score_conv(p)
                        if np.isfinite(sc):
                            update_by_n(best_by_n, m, p, c, sc)
                            update_global_state(global_state, sc, p, c, m, beta)

    # ---------- A few asymmetric two-sided mixtures ----------
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 75:
        items = sorted(best_by_n.items(), key=candidate_key)
        top_ns = [int(n) for n, _ in items[:6]]
        if not top_ns:
            top_ns = [128, 256, 512]
        for n in top_ns:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30:
                break
            base_betas = (float(global_state[4]), 0.50, 0.54, 0.58)
            for beta in base_betas:
                if not (0.35 <= beta <= 0.85):
                    continue
                for w in (0.15, 0.30, 0.70, 0.85):
                    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20:
                        break
                    p = mixed_power_sequence(n, beta, w)
                    c, sc = score_conv(p)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        update_by_n(best_by_n, n, p, c, sc)
                        update_global_state(global_state, sc, p, c, n, betaa if False else beta)

    # ---------- Local search on a few diverse best candidates ----------
    items = sorted(best_by_n.items(), key=candidate_key)
    local_candidates = []
    seen_ns = set()
    for n, data in items:
        if n in seen_ns:
            continue
        if data[1] is None:
            continue
        local_candidates.append((int(n), data[1].copy(), data[2].copy(), float(data[0])))
        seen_ns.add(n)
        if len(local_candidates) >= 6:
            break

    if len(local_candidates) > 0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 40:
        count = len(local_candidates)
        for idx in range(count):
            left = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if left <= 20:
                break
            chunk = max(2.0, left / max(1, count - idx))
            local_deadline = time.monotonic() + chunk
            n, p, c, sc = local_candidates[idx]
            p2, c2, sc2 = local_search(p, c, sc, local_deadline, rng, label=f"n={n}")
            if p2 is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(p2)) and np.isfinite(sc2):
                update_by_n(best_by_n, n, p2, c2, sc2)
                update_global_state(global_state, sc2, p2, c2, n, float(global_state[4]))

    # ---------- Exploit support reduction / truncation ----------
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 15:
        if global_state[1] is not None:
            try_truncation_candidates(global_state[1], best_by_n, global_state)

        # truncate top few lengths individually
        items = sorted(best_by_n.items(), key=candidate_key)
        for n, data in items[:8]:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5:
                break
            try_truncation_candidates(data[1], best_by_n, global_state)

    # ---------- Final official validation and selection ----------
    final_arrays = []

    if global_state[1] is not None:
        final_arrays.append(("global", global_state[1], float(global_state[0])))

    # Add current best per length (these are safe copies)
    for n, data in sorted(best_by_n.items(), key=candidate_key)[:20]:
        if data[1] is not None:
            final_arrays.append((f"len{n}", data[1], float(data[0])))

    # Add a handful of extremely safe pure power fallbacks.
    fallbacks = [
        (96, 0.52), (128, 0.52), (128, 0.54), (192, 0.53),
        (256, 0.53), (384, 0.53), (512, 0.52), (512, 0.54)
    ]
    for n, b in fallbacks:
        q = power_sequence(n, b)
        final_arrays.append((f"fallback{n}_{b}", q, np.inf))

    best_seq_out = None
    best_score_out = np.inf

    for name, arr, _ in final_arrays:
        if arr is None:
            continue
        try:
            a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if a.size == 0:
                continue
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
            a = a[np.isfinite(a)]
            if a.size == 0:
                continue
            seq = [float(x) for x in a]
            sc = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score_out:
                best_score_out = sc
                best_seq_out = seq
        except Exception:
            continue

    if best_seq_out is None:
        # Absolute fallback: short positive sequence with finite official score.
        best_seq_out = [1.0]
        best_score_out = np.inf

    # Print a concise log for the caller / future inspection.
    try:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start_time
        print(f"Final candidate: length={len(best_seq_out)}, internal_best=", end="")
        if np.isfinite(global_state[0]):
            print(f"{global_state[0]:.9f}", end="")
        else:
            print("inf", end="")
        print(f", official_best={best_score_out if np.isfinite(best_score_out) else 'inf'}, elapsed={elapsed:.1f}s")
    except Exception:
        pass

    return [float(x) for x in best_seq_out]
```
